Plant-microbe interactions and immunityGrowthAnalytical methods : advancing methods and applications · 2026-10-07
matched: pathogen*, development, biosensor*
abstract

In recent years, gold nanoparticle-based biosensors have emerged as a rapid, specific, easy, and cost-effective alternative for detecting human, plant, and animal pathogens. Klebsiella pneumoniae (K. pneumoniae) is one of the most common drug-resistant pathogenic strains that cause nosocomial infections and outbreaks. Therefore, efforts to address the disease have focused on development of novel diagnostic technologies. The present study highlights the development of a sensitive electrochemical DNA biosensor for K. pneumoniae detection, fabricated on a glassy carbon electrode (GCE) modified with gold nanoparticles/reduced graphene oxide (AuNPs/rGO). Using greener methods to synthesize AuNPs promotes sustainability and enhances biocompatibility and environmental safety. By using an indicator-free approach, the current biosensor identifies K. pneumoniae infection and further minimizes the need for additional dyes to function as hybridization indicators. Square wave voltammetry (SWV) was used to characterize DNA immobilization and hybridization. The limit of detection (LOD) of the biosensor for the target K. pneumoniae DNA sequence was 0.16 pM with a detection range of 0.01-100 pM. The DNA biosensor facilitates rapid detection of target K. pneumoniae DNA, with 2-5 minutes of detection time. The DNA biosensor demonstrated high specificity in distinguishing complementary DNA sequences from interfering species. The results indicated that the AuNPs/rGO nanocomposite was a promising substrate for the development of high-throughput electrocatalysts for K. pneumoniae determination.

6.5 Deep Learning and Domain Expertise for Fabricating High-Performance Biosensors.
Xiao ML, Zhang P, Li Z … Liu JZ.
GrowthACS sensors · 2026-10-15
matched: development, biosensor*, deep learning
abstract

Transcription factor-based biosensors are essential for the scalable bioproduction of plant natural products. However, a major bottleneck in developing high-performance biosensors is the engineering of responsive promoters due to the vast sequence space and poorly understood sequence-function relationships. Here, we extend our previously published DeepSEED framework into an artificial intelligence-driven pipeline for efficient and extensible biosensor-responsive promoter optimization. This strategy integrates deep learning with domain-specific biological expertise. By fixing core promoter elements, varying the number and position of transcription factor binding sites (TFBSs) to define promoter backbones, and subsequently optimizing the flanking sequences, we enable the de novo design of promoters. We systematically apply this strategy to optimize a panel of biosensors across different transcription factors and regulatory modes, including four metabolite-responsive biosensors and a quorum-sensing-based environmental sensor. The resulting biosensors exhibit diverse dynamic behaviors with markedly improved sensitivity, expanded dynamic range, and increased maximum output. For instance, the optimized naringenin biosensor demonstrates a 32-fold broader dynamic range and a 27-fold higher maximum output compared to its parent. To illustrate its utility in metabolic engineering, we employed a DeepSEED-designed promoter to dynamically regulate a p-coumaric acid biosynthesis pathway, boosting production by 117.0%. Our work extends the previously published DeepSEED framework into a powerful pipeline for biosensor optimization and delivers a portfolio of high-performance regulatory elements, addressing a critical need and facilitating the development of more intelligent and productive metabolic engineering systems.

6.5 EsPIP5K4, a target of Es-miR408, positively regulates salt tolerance in Siberian wildrye.
De Y, Li D, Gao F … Wu Z.
Abiotic stressGrowthPlant science : an international journal of experimental plant biology · 2026-10-23
matched: salt stress, stoma*, reporter*, luciferase*
abstract

Siberian wildrye (Elymus sibiricus L.) is a native grass of significant ecological and forage value for ecological restoration and livestock production in western China. However, the miRNA-mediated regulatory mechanisms underlying its salt tolerance remain largely unexplored. In this study, through integrative miRNA-mRNA profiling under salt stress, we identified EsPIP5K4 as a candidate target of Es-miR408. The inverse expression patterns of Es-miR408 (downregulated) and EsPIP5K4 (upregulated) under salt stress, together with dual-luciferase reporter assays, indicated that Es-miR408 negatively regulates EsPIP5K4 through its 3'UTR. Functional validation using TRV-mediated virus-induced gene silencing (VIGS) of EsPIP5K4 in Siberian wildrye significantly compromised salt tolerance, as evidenced by greater malondialdehyde (MDA) accumulation, reduced proline increase, and accelerated chlorophyll degradation under salt stress. Conversely, heterologous overexpression of EsPIP5K4 in tobacco conferred enhanced salt tolerance, accompanied by improved physiological performance and altered stomatal traits under salt stress. Collectively, our findings support a model in which salt stress downregulates Es-miR408, thereby relieving repression of EsPIP5K4 and contributing to salt tolerance. This study not only reveals a novel Es-miR408-EsPIP5K4 regulatory module for salt adaptation in Siberian wildrye, but also provides a potential genetic target for molecular breeding of salt-tolerant forage grasses.

6.5 Surface-Engineered Dual-Emission of Carbon Dots for Ratiometric Detection of Hydroxyl Radicals.
Pan Y, Wang S, Zhang W … Guo L.
GrowthNanophotonics (Berlin, Germany) · 2026-05-01
matched: development, ratiometric
abstract

Carbon dots (CDs) have emerged as versatile photoluminescent nanomaterials with exceptional optical properties and biocompatibility, demonstrating considerable potential in bioimaging, energy conversion, and sensing applications. However, the development of aqueous CDs with tunable red dual-emission remains challenging owing to limitations in electronic energy-level alignment and ongoing debates concerning the photoluminescence mechanisms. Herein, we report a facile hydrothermal synthesis strategy employing trehalose as the carbon precursor and sulfuric acid serving as both an oxidation regulator and a polymerization agent to regulate the distribution of surface states and endow the CDs with dual emission properties. The resulting CDs exhibit well-resolved tunable dual emission bands around 450 and 590 nm, attributed to core states and surface-oxidized states, respectively. Time-resolved spectroscopic investigations reveal that the engineered surface motifs effectively facilitate ultrafast electron migration from the carbon core to the oxidized states. Intriguingly, hydroxyl radicals induce selective static quenching of the surface-oxidation-associated red emission, endowing CDs-based sensors with ultralow detection limits, self-calibration capabilities, and real-time visualization through ratiometric performance. Furthermore, the as-prepared unique CDs have been validated for the reliable quantification of hydroxyl radical levels in plant onion cells under oxidative stress.

6.5 The VcNAC072-VcF3'5'H4 module regulates anthocyanin biosynthesis in blueberry: molecular mechanism.
Li Q, Zhao T, Yan T … Wang D.
Kinases and signal transductionPlant-microbe interactions and immunityPlant physiology and biochemistry : PPB · 2026-10-01
matched: signal transduction, immun*, reporter*, luciferase*
abstract

Light intensity is a pivotal environmental factor governing anthocyanin accumulation in plants; however, the molecular mechanism by which it regulates anthocyanin biosynthesis in blueberry (Vaccinium corymbosum L.) remains elusive. Combined transcriptomic and metabolomic analyses revealed that VcF3'5'H4, a gene encoding flavonoid 3',5'-hydroxylase (F3'5'H), is closely associated with anthocyanin accumulation in blueberry. Light treatment significantly induces VcF3'5'H4 transcription, and its expression level is strongly positively correlated with anthocyanin content. Overexpression of VcF3'5'H4 in blueberry callus markedly promotes anthocyanin biosynthesis. Dual-luciferase reporter, yeast one-hybrid, chromatin immunoprecipitation coupled with quantitative polymerase chain reaction (ChIP-qPCR), and electrophoretic mobility shift assay (EMSA) assays confirmed that the transcription factor VcNAC072 specifically binds to the VcF3'5'H4 promoter and functions as a transcriptional activator to positively regulate its expression. Overexpression or silencing of VcNAC072 in blueberry callus, tobacco and Arabidopsis thaliana plants significantly alters VcF3'5'H4 transcription and anthocyanin accumulation. Further investigations demonstrated that the key light-responsive transcription factor VcHY5 directly targets the VcNAC072 promoter and enhances its transcriptional activity under light conditions. Collectively, this study elucidates the regulatory mechanism by which the VcHY5-VcNAC072-VcF3'5'H4 molecular module mediates light-induced anthocyanin biosynthesis in blueberry, providing novel theoretical insights into the coordinated regulation of light signal transduction, secondary metabolism, and stress adaptation in plants.

6.0 A dual-readout ratiometric fluorescence-smartphone assay for rapid preliminary screening of tetracycline-class residues in milk.
Yu C, Guo X, Song Z … Li W.
Talanta · 2026-10-15
matched: ratiometric
abstract

Tetracycline-class antibiotic residues in milk raise food safety concerns, requiring rapid, reliable preliminary screening of pretreated samples before instrumental confirmation. In this work, a dual-readout ratiometric fluorescence sensing platform based on a citrate-mediated biomass-derived PCDs/Eu3+ coordination sensing system (PCDs/Eu sensing system) was developed for rapid preliminary screening of tetracycline-class residues in pretreated milk samples. In this system, potato flour-derived carbon dots (PCDs) exhibit blue emission at 432 nm, while tetracycline antibiotics sensitize Eu3+ emission at 622 nm. Mechanistic studies using TC as a representative analyte supported predominantly inner-filter-related attenuation of the blue emission and coordination-assisted antenna sensitization of the red emission, generating a concentration-dependent ratio (F622/F432) and a blue-to-red transition. Under optimized conditions in buffer, the method exhibited linear ranges of 0.2-100 μM and calculated detection limits of 8.9 ± 0.1 nM (TC), 3.8 ± 0.09 nM (OTC), and 9.8 ± 0.12 nM (DOC). The assay provides dual quantitative readouts through fluorescence spectrometry and smartphone RGB analysis, while immobilization on paper strips enables portable visual and smartphone-assisted screening. Validation in spiked samples of six commercial milk products yielded recoveries of 95.10-105.91%, RSDs ≤3.57%, and matrix effects of -7.77% to +7.08%. Comparison with LC-MS/MS in spiked milk samples supports the assay's feasibility for preliminary screening within the tested concentration range Integrating biomass-derived carbon dots, Eu3+ coordination, smartphone quantification, and paper strips, this ratiometric platform supports portable screening readout for pretreated milk.

6.0 Performance uncertainty in medical image analysis: A large-scale investigation of confidence intervals.
André P, Heitz C, Christodoulou E … Colliot O.
Trees and woody plantsMedical image analysis · 2026-10-12
matched: tree, image analysis, segmentation, machine learning
abstract

Performance uncertainty quantification is essential for reliable validation and eventual clinical translation of medical imaging artificial intelligence (AI). Confidence intervals (CIs) play a central role in this process by indicating how precise a reported performance estimate is. Yet, due to the limited amount of work examining CI behavior in medical imaging, the community remains largely unaware of how many diverse CI methods exist and how they behave in specific settings. The purpose of this study is to close this gap. To this end, we conducted a large-scale empirical analysis across a total of 24 segmentation and classification tasks, using 19 trained models per task group, a broad spectrum of commonly used performance metrics, multiple aggregation strategies, and several widely adopted CI methods. Reliability (coverage) and precision (width) of each CI method were estimated across all settings to characterize their dependence on study characteristics. Our analysis revealed five principal findings: (1) the sample size required for reliable CIs varies from a few dozens to several thousands of cases depending on study parameters; (2) CI behavior is strongly affected by the choice of performance metric; (3) aggregation strategy substantially influences the reliability of CIs, e.g. they require more observations for macro than for micro; (4) the machine learning problem (segmentation versus classification) modulates these effects; (5) different CI methods are not equally reliable and precise depending on the use case. Finally, we derived practical implications of this study in the form of a decision tree which shall prove useful to the community. This paves the way for future consensus guidelines on reporting performance uncertainty.

6.0 Ratiometric fluorescent immunoassay mediated by alkaline phosphatase-catalyzed Eu3+ coordination polymers for the detection of Ochratoxin A.
Guo H, Zhao Y, Ji S … Zhou Y.
Kinases and signal transductionPlant-microbe interactions and immunityFood chemistry · 2026-10-10
matched: phosphatase*, immun*, ratiometric
abstract

Ochratoxin A (OTA) contamination severely threatens global food safety. Herein, a ratiometric fluorescent immunoassay method was established for trace OTA detection based on an alkaline phosphatase (ALP)-catalyzed Eu3+ coordination polymer (Eu-CP). The Eu-CP probe is self-assembled from ciprofloxacin, adenosine monophosphate, and Eu3+. Upon activation by the Mg2+ cofactor, ALP drives the dephosphorylation and subsequent structural disassembly of the Eu-CP framework, which synchronously regulates dual-emission fluorescent signals. In indirect competitive reaction, OTA competes with bovine serum albumin-OTA for limited antibodies, and the bound ALP-labeled secondary antibody is inversely correlated with OTA concentration. Based on the ratiometric fluorescence signal (F618/F418), the proposed method exhibited a reliable linear range of 0.4883-62.5 ng/mL (R2 = 0.9909) and a limit of detection as low as 0.0273 ng/mL. Recoveries in corn, oat and rice were 97.07%-119.11% with inter-batch CV < 10%. This study provides a simple and sensitive method for trace OTA detection.
